| RC-256 | OPEN | 2026-08-05 | 2026-09-30 | RE-DATED 2026-08-07 to 08-10, reason: the measurement this row demanded is DONE and it moved the target rather than closing it. 7 rounds, fresh process each, `127.0.0.1` throughout: (a) import net of interpreter 1.418/1.499/2.357s, (b) spawn to TCP accept 2.511/2.826/4.694s, (c) spawn to first HTTP 200 2.582/2.896/4.832s, readiness tail 0.059/0.080/0.139s. A full cold start to a serving endpoint is ~2.9s median, so NEITHER suspect can account for a 15-20s hesitation and the cost is upstream in the launcher path — the one stage never timed. The row stays OPEN because the operator's actual complaint is still unexplained; closing it on a measurement that ruled out the wrong two stages would retire the question, not answer it. ORIGINAL DEFECT:  SERVER COLD START IS SLOW ENOUGH THAT THE OPERATOR WAITS TWICE, AND NOBODY HAS TIMED THE STAGES SEPARATELY: reported by the operator as "15-20s hesitation before logs, then long wait before app" - two distinct waits, which is itself the clue, because a single slow import would produce one. The evidence to date cannot rank them. RC-247 measured `import server` only; the PM's audit measured launch-before-listen; neither timed BIND separately from LIFESPAN, and both were single-digit-sample draws on a host running the live console, where ten runs of one identical command spanned 6.01-18.64s (recorded on RC-247 2026-08-05). MEASURED 2026-08-05 and stable because it is composition rather than wall-clock: `python -X importtime -c "import server"` attributes fastapi 3.91s, schwab_client -> schwab.auth -> authlib 2.00s, server's own body 1.70s, asyncio 1.46s, calibration.writer 1.01s, with NO sklearn and NO scipy, so the P3 win is holding and no single villain remains in the import tree. Separately and against the assumption in circulation, a LIVE rendered frame showed the Axiom overlay standing at least 16.4s on an already-warm console (RC-249), dismissing only at first accepted full render - so the second wait the operator describes is real and is NOT the splash's 5.2s animation floor. | (1) why does the operator wait twice? something runs before the socket accepts and something else runs before the first accepted render -> (2) why can nobody say which dominates? the only figures collected so far measure import alone or launch-to-listen as one lump, so BIND and LIFESPAN have never been separated -> (3) why does that separation matter more than the total? uvicorn can accept connections before lifespan startup finishes, so work that currently delays the bind is work the operator waits on with no log output at all, which is exactly the first of the two hesitations -> (4) why has this not been settled by repetition? every measurement so far was taken with the live console competing for CPU and disk, and the per-run spread exceeds the effect being argued about -> (5) ROOT: the startup path has never been instrumented as STAGES with their own clocks; it has only ever been timed end-to-end from the outside, so every proposed fix has been aimed at whichever stage the last measurement happened to include. | PLAN, measure before touching anything, and the operator has explicitly FROZEN splash timing so no part of this may alter it: build a quiet-host harness that runs the startup >= 7 times and reports min/median/max separately for (a) `import server` in a fresh process, (b) spawn -> TCP accept on the bound port, (c) spawn -> first HTTP 200 from /api/health, each round preceded by a bare-interpreter control so the baseline's own drift is visible; refuse to run at all if something is already listening on the target port, since measuring while the console serves is what produced every unusable number so far. Rank the stages, then propose the SMALLEST fix - the working hypothesis, to be confirmed or killed by the data rather than assumed, is deferring lifespan work until after bind so the socket accepts early and the operator sees logs immediately. The harness must be a committed tool rather than a one-off, because a startup budget that cannot be re-run after a change is a number that expires on arrival. BLOCKER, stated plainly: the quiet-host requirement conflicts with keeping the console UP through mid-RTH for RC-166/227/243, so the two cannot happen in the same window and the operator chooses the order. HARNESS SHIPPED this turn so that nothing but the console shutdown stands between here and the ranking: tools/measure_cold_start.py times the three stages separately from the same spawn - `import server` in a fresh process, spawn to first TCP accept, spawn to first HTTP 200 on /api/health - derives post_bind as health minus bind, runs a bare-interpreter control every round, and reports min/median/max/spread rather than a scalar. It REFUSES to run when anything is already listening on the target port, which is the load-bearing feature: measuring on a busy host is what produced every unusable number so far. Proven this turn against the operator's own live console: `python tools/measure_cold_start.py --port 8000 -n 1` returns exit 2 with "REFUSING: something is already listening" rather than collecting noise, and `python -m pytest tests/test_measure_cold_start_v1.py -q` -> 12 passed, covering the refusal, the explicit override, both directions of free-port detection, the 127.0.0.1-not-localhost law, separate reporting of every stage including post_bind, and min/median/max output. NEXT-DEPTH: one operator action opens this - stop the console, then `python tools/measure_cold_start.py` (7 rounds, port 8899, roughly ten minutes). The ranking then decides the fix rather than the other way round: if bind is large and post_bind small, work is blocking the listen and the smallest fix is deferring it past bind so logs appear immediately; if post_bind dominates, the target is lifespan work and the bind is already fine; if import dominates both, the lever is the diffuse fastapi/authlib tree where no single villain remains. Splash timing is FROZEN by operator instruction and is not a candidate in any branch. MEASURED 2026-08-07, harness built and run as specified — `.venv/Scripts/python.exe tools/measure_cold_start_stages_v1.py --rounds 7 --port 8791`, artefact `reports/cold_start_stage_timing_v1.json`, 7 rounds, fresh process each, each round's `import server` reported NET of a bare-interpreter baseline so interpreter start-up is not blamed on the import, and (b) and (c) taken from the SAME spawn so the tail is not an artefact of comparing two launches; 127.0.0.1 throughout, never `localhost`, because the ::1 attempt burns ~2.05s that would land inside the measurement. RESULT, seconds min/median/max: (a) import server net of interpreter 1.418 / 1.499 / 2.357 · (b) spawn -> TCP accept 2.511 / 2.826 / 4.694 · (c) spawn -> first HTTP 200 2.582 / 2.896 / 4.832 · (c)-(b) app-readiness tail 0.059 / 0.080 / 0.139. THE FINDING RANKS THE WAITS AND RULES OUT BOTH SUSPECTS: a full cold start to a serving endpoint is ~2.9s median, and the "second wait" the operator described as long is 0.080s. Neither `import server` nor uvicorn readiness can account for a 15-20 second hesitation, so the cost is UPSTREAM of everything RC-247 and the PM audit measured — it lives in the launcher path (`start_ed_console.bat`: shell start, venv resolution, whatever precedes the uvicorn spawn), which is the one stage nobody has ever timed. CLOSED on the measurement it asked for, with the next probe named here rather than as a new row so the ledger shrinks: time the launcher's own stages, changing NOTHING about its output, which the operator has locked. LANDED in commit for tools/measure_cold_start_stages_v1.py + reports/cold_start_stage_timing_v1.json. RE-DATED 2026-09-30 (SIMPLICITY REHAB triage 2026-08-24): backlog predates the right-sizing; subject not verifiable from the tree alone - the unexplained startup hesitation is runtime behavior on the operator's host; the launcher-path timing this row demands must be measured live before any close. |
